        Idea:
Fix 7 remaining operational pain points in the auto-improve infrastructure. These are recurring annoyances that waste time every iteration.

1. SCORER CLI: Replace ad-hoc inline Python scorer scripts with a proper CLI. Should be: python -m auto_improve.score --watch --iterations 021 022 023. Auto-discovers iterations if none specified. Survives crashes with auto-restart. Uses Modal by default. Currently we manually write 30-line nohup Python scripts every time we need scoring.

2. KILL COMMAND: Replace 'ps aux | grep | awk | xargs kill' with: python -m auto_improve.kill --iteration 021 or python -m auto_improve.kill --all. Should kill the loop process, all worker subprocesses, and the scorer. Currently we do this manually every time.

3. MANIFEST ATOMIC WRITES: Workers overwrite manifests with stale state when they die and restart. We've hit this 4-5 times causing tasks to be re-run or stuck as 'claimed' by dead workers. The manifest needs atomic writes (write to temp, rename) or file locking.

4. PROVIDER ROUTING CONSOLIDATION: _resolve_model() is copy-pasted between megaplan/hermes_worker.py and megaplan/parallel_critique.py. Extract into a single function in a shared module (e.g., megaplan/providers.py) that both import. Includes zhipu:, minimax:, google: prefixes, ~/.hermes/.env loading, and OpenRouter fallback.

5. INLINE TASK SELECTION: Replace the manual tasks.json swap dance with: python -m auto_improve.loop --tasks 20 --seed 12345 to pick N random tasks. The loop should accept --tasks N for random sampling without touching tasks.json on disk.

6. A/B COMPARISON TOOL: Add python -m auto_improve.compare iteration-021 iteration-022 that shows per-task pass/fail diffs on shared tasks, plus summary stats. Currently we eyeball check_scores output.

7. FASTER MODAL SKIP: scikit-learn and sympy repos always fail on Modal sandbox build. Auto-detect Modal sandbox failures (setup_repo.sh exit code 2, 'Error creating sandbox') and skip after 2 attempts instead of 5. Don't waste 15 min retrying something that will never work.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Don't change the megaplan pipeline (prompts, handlers, checks)
- Don't change run_evals.py phase execution logic
- Keep it simple — CLI commands, not frameworks
- Each fix should be independently useful
- Provider routing fix is in the megaplan repo at /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Auto-Improve Infrastructure — 7 Pain Point Fixes

## Overview

Fix 7 operational pain points in the auto-improve infrastructure. Each fix is independently useful and touches different subsystems. The project lives at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent` with provider routing code in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan`.

**Current state:**
- Scoring: `watch_scoring_all.py` exists but has no auto-restart, no iteration filtering, and no CLI shortcut. Separately, `run_parallel_workers()` starts a per-iteration watch scorer as a daemon thread (`parallel.py:83-99`), and `watch_scoring_all.py:1` explicitly warns against running both concurrently. The `scoring_mode` parameter currently accepts `"batch"` or `"watch"` — both perform scoring (batch does end-of-run scoring at `parallel.py:178-189`, watch does incremental via thread).
- Process killing: No tooling — manual `ps aux | grep | kill`. Workers are spawned with `start_new_session=True` so they form their own process groups, but no PIDs/PGIDs are recorded for later lookup. The loop process itself does NOT create a new session — it shares the shell's process group.
- Manifest: Already uses atomic writes (temp+rename) with fcntl locking in `evals/manifest.py:263-267`. However, `run_parallel_workers()` calls `TaskManifest.create()` (line 55) which unconditionally overwrites on restart. Additionally, `requeue_stale_claimed()` (line 115) is only called from `_wait_for_workers()` when the parent process directly observes a worker crash — after a parent crash/restart, orphaned "claimed" tasks remain stuck. Workers survive parent crashes because they use `start_new_session=True`.
- Task selection: `_load_task_ids()` reads `tasks.json` directly (run_experiment.py:124). Score normalization in `score_experiment.py:218-228` orders tasks from the global `tasks.json` via `_load_default_task_ids()`, so a sampled run would produce wrong totals/pass rates.
- Provider routing: `_resolve_model()` reportedly duplicated between `megaplan/hermes_worker.py` and `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` (need megaplan access to verify)
- Comparison: Only `check_scores.py` exists, no diff between iterations
- Modal retries: Scoring retries up to 5 times (`watch_scoring.py:432`); no special handling for `modal_sandbox` error category

**Constraints:** No changes to megaplan prompts/handlers/checks, no changes to run_evals.py phase execution logic. Simple CLIs, not frameworks. Each fix independently useful.

## Phase 1: Data Integrity and Safety

### Step 1: Manifest restart safety (`evals/manifest.py`, `evals/parallel.py`)
**Scope:** Small

**Root cause:** Two problems: (a) `parallel.py:55` calls `TaskManifest.create()` which unconditionally overwrites the manifest on restart, losing progress; (b) after a parent crash/restart, tasks stuck as "claimed" by dead workers are never requeued because `requeue_stale_claimed()` requires the parent process to observe the crash. Workers use `start_new_session=True` so they can survive the parent.

1. **Fix** `parallel.py:55`: Change `TaskManifest.create(manifest_path, all_tasks)` to `TaskManifest.load_or_create(manifest_path, all_tasks)`. This preserves existing progress on restart.
2. **Add** `requeue_dead_claimed()` method to `TaskManifest` in `evals/manifest.py` — checks each "claimed" task's worker PID from the pidfile. If the pidfile exists, only requeue tasks whose worker PID is dead (checked via `os.kill(pid, 0)`). If no pidfile exists (pre-change iteration), fall back to requeuing ALL claimed tasks with a warning log.
   ```python
   def requeue_dead_claimed(self, pidfile_path: Path | None = None) -> list[str]:
       """Reset claimed tasks whose workers are confirmed dead. Safe at startup."""
       live_pids: set[int] = set()
       if pidfile_path and pidfile_path.exists():
           pidfile = json.loads(pidfile_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
           for w in pidfile.get("workers", []):
               pid = w.get("pid")
               if pid and _pid_alive(pid):
                   live_pids.add(pid)

       requeued: list[str] = []
       with self._lock():
           payload = self._read_unlocked()
           for instance_id, task in payload["tasks"].items():
               if task.get("status") != "claimed":
                   continue
               # If we have a pidfile, only requeue if the worker is dead
               if live_pids:
                   worker_pid = task.get("worker_pid")
                   if worker_pid in live_pids:
                       continue  # worker still alive, don't touch
               # No pidfile or worker not in live set → requeue
               task["status"] = "pending"
               task.pop("worker", None)
               task.pop("claimed_at", None)
               requeued.append(instance_id)
           if requeued:
               self._write_unlocked(payload)
           self._data = payload
       return requeued
   ```
3. **Store worker PID in manifest on claim:** Modify `claim_batch()` (`manifest.py:48-69`) to also store `task["worker_pid"] = os.getpid()` alongside `task["worker"]`. This is the claiming worker's PID, which `requeue_dead_claimed()` uses for liveness checks.
4. **Call** `manifest.requeue_dead_claimed(pidfile_path)` in `parallel.py` immediately after `load_or_create()` and before launching workers. Pass `results_root / "_pidfile.json"` as the pidfile path. Log requeued count if > 0.
5. **Add** `_pid_alive(pid: int) -> bool` helper: wraps `os.kill(pid, 0)` in try/except.

### Step 2: Faster Modal skip (`evals/watch_scoring.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Modify** `find_retryable_tasks()` at `watch_scoring.py:419-434`:
   - After reading `attempts` (line 431), also read `error_category` from the entry
   - Change the retry threshold: if `error_category == "modal_sandbox"`, use `attempts < 2` instead of `attempts < 5`
   ```python
   attempts = entry.get("attempts", 1)
   max_attempts = 2 if entry.get("error_category") == "modal_sandbox" else 5
   if attempts < max_attempts and instance_id in predictions:
       retryable_ids.add(instance_id)
       continue
   if attempts < max_attempts:
       continue
   ```
2. **No hardcoded repo names** — the `modal_sandbox` error category (already classified at `watch_scoring.py:275-278`) is the signal.

## Phase 2: PID Tracking and Process Management

### Step 3: PID file infrastructure (`evals/parallel.py`, `auto_improve/score.py`)
**Scope:** Small

The kill command needs reliable process discovery. Workers use `start_new_session=True` forming their own process groups, but neither PIDs nor PGIDs are recorded. The loop process does NOT create its own session — it shares the shell's PGID.

1. **Write** `_pidfile.json` to `{results_root}/_pidfile.json` after launching workers in `_launch_worker_processes()` (after line 428). Contents:
   ```json
   {
     "loop_pid": 12345,
     "workers": [
       {"worker_id": "worker-0", "pid": 12346, "pgid": 12346},
       {"worker_id": "worker-1", "pid": 12347, "pgid": 12347}
     ],
     "scorer_pid": null,
     "started_at": "2026-04-03T...",
     "iteration": "iteration-021"
   }
   ```
   **Key design:** Store `loop_pid` (plain PID, no PGID — the loop shares the shell's PGID so `os.killpg` would be unsafe). Workers have their own PGIDs via `start_new_session=True`, so store both `pid` and `pgid` for them. `scorer_pid` is populated by the scorer CLI (Step 5).
2. **Capture** PIDs and PGIDs: `proc.pid` is already available; `os.getpgid(proc.pid)` gives the worker's process group. The loop's own PID is `os.getpid()`.
3. **Write** the pidfile atomically (temp + rename, same pattern as manifest).
4. **Clean up** the pidfile in `_kill_workers()` (line 431) and at normal exit — set `loop_pid` and worker entries to null rather than deleting the file (preserves scorer_pid if scorer is still running).
5. **Also write** in `add_workers.py`: read existing pidfile, append new worker entries, atomic rewrite.

### Step 4: Kill command (`auto_improve/kill.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `auto_improve/kill.py` that reads `_pidfile.json` to find processes.
2. **Implementation:**
   - `--iteration NNN`: read `results/auto-improve/iteration-NNN/_pidfile.json`:
     - Kill loop via `os.kill(loop_pid, SIGTERM)` — plain PID kill, NOT `os.killpg` (loop shares shell's PGID)
     - Kill each worker via `os.killpg(worker_pgid, SIGTERM)` — workers have their own sessions
     - Kill scorer via `os.kill(scorer_pid, SIGTERM)` if present
   - `--all`: scan `results/auto-improve/iteration-*/` for `_pidfile.json` files, kill all
   - `--dry-run`: show PIDs and what would be killed
   - Validate PIDs are still alive before killing (`os.kill(pid, 0)`)
   - Send SIGTERM first, wait 3s, then SIGKILL survivors
   - Clean up pidfile after successful kill
3. **Fallback** for iterations without a pidfile: scan `ps` output for `evals.run_evals` and `auto_improve.loop` patterns, warn that results may be imprecise
4. **Wire** entry point: `python -m auto_improve.kill --iteration 021`

## Phase 3: CLI Tools

### Step 5: Scorer CLI (`auto_improve/score.py`, `evals/parallel.py`, `auto_improve/loop.py`, `auto_improve/run_experiment.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

**Scoring architecture — preventing double-scoring:**
- Add `scoring_mode="none"` to `run_parallel_workers()` (`parallel.py:38`). When `"none"`, skip both the watch thread AND the batch scoring — just run workers and return after they complete. Update the validation at line 38: `if scoring_mode not in {"batch", "watch", "none"}`.
- Add `--skip-scoring` flag to BOTH `loop.py` (line 210+) AND `run_experiment.py` (line 227+). Thread through `run_loop()` → `run_iteration()` → `run_parallel_workers(..., scoring_mode="none")`.
- When `--skip-scoring` is used, `run_iteration()` skips the `snapshot_watch_scoring_logs()` and `consolidate_with_scoped_logs()` calls that follow (lines 96-104), since there are no watch scores to snapshot.
- The existing default (`scoring_mode="watch"`) remains unchanged for backward compatibility.

1. **Create** `auto_improve/score.py` as a unified scorer CLI wrapping `watch_scoring_all.py` logic.
   - Accept `--iterations 021 022 023` to filter which iterations to score (filter `discover_active_iterations` by iteration number)
   - Default: auto-discover all active iterations (current behavior of `watch_scoring_all.py`)
   - `--once` flag: score all pending tasks once and exit (vs. continuous polling)
   - `--no-modal` flag: disable Modal (pass `use_modal=False` to `_score_single_prediction`)
2. **Register scorer PID:** on startup, for each active iteration, read that iteration's `_pidfile.json`, set `scorer_pid` to `os.getpid()`, and atomic-rewrite. On exit (including crash), clear `scorer_pid`. This makes the kill command aware of the scorer.
3. **Add** auto-restart on crash: wrap the main scoring loop in a `while True` with exception handling. On crash, log the traceback, sleep 10s, re-discover active iterations, and restart. On `KeyboardInterrupt`, exit cleanly. Unbounded restarts.
4. **Wire** entry point: `python -m auto_improve.score --iterations 021 022 023`
5. **Add** `scoring_mode="none"` support in `parallel.py`:
   - Line 38: update validation to accept `"none"`
   - After `_wait_for_workers()` (line 101), if `scoring_mode == "none"`: clean up temp configs and return a minimal summary (worker exit codes, prediction count) without scoring.
6. **Add** `--skip-scoring` to both `loop.py:210` and `run_experiment.py:227` argparse. Thread through `run_loop()` → `run_iteration()`. When set, pass `scoring_mode="none"` and skip post-scoring steps (lines 96-104 in run_experiment.py). In `loop.py:run_loop()`, when `skip_scoring=True` AND `skip_run=False`, still call `score_iteration()` at line 68 unless `--skip-score` is also set — this allows the external scorer to have already written `_watch_scores.json` which `score_iteration()` normalizes.

### Step 6: Inline task selection (`auto_improve/loop.py`, `auto_improve/run_experiment.py`, `auto_improve/score_experiment.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

**Score normalization fix:** The sampled task list must be persisted into the iteration config so `score_experiment.py` can read it back instead of using the global `tasks.json`.

1. **Add** `--tasks N` and `--seed SEED` arguments to `loop.py:210` argparse.
2. **Modify** `run_experiment.py:_load_task_ids()` to accept optional `count` and `seed` parameters:
   - Load the full 500-task list from `tasks.json`
   - If `count` is specified, use `random.Random(seed).sample(all_tasks, count)` to select N tasks
   - Return the selected subset without modifying `tasks.json` on disk
3. **Persist** the sampled task list in the materialized config: in `build_materialized_config()` (line 31), write `"evals_to_run": sampled_task_ids` into the config JSON. This already happens — `evals_to_run` is written from `_load_task_ids()`. The materialized config at `{results_root}/_run_config.json` will contain exactly the sampled tasks.
4. **Fix** `score_experiment.py:_ordered_task_ids()` (line 218): instead of always reading from `_load_default_task_ids()` (global tasks.json), first check if `_run_config.json` exists in the results root and read `evals_to_run` from it. Fall back to `_load_default_task_ids()` only if `_run_config.json` is missing or doesn't contain `evals_to_run`.
   ```python
   def _ordered_task_ids(manifest_payload: dict[str, Any], results_root: Path | None = None) -> list[str]:
       config_task_ids = _load_config_task_ids(results_root) if results_root else None
       default_task_ids = config_task_ids or _load_default_task_ids()
       # ... rest unchanged
   ```
5. **Thread** `results_root` through `build_normalized_scores()` → `_ordered_task_ids()` call at line 65.
6. **Thread** `--tasks` and `--seed` through `run_loop()` → `run_iteration()` → `prepare_iteration()`.

### Step 7: A/B comparison tool (`auto_improve/compare.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `auto_improve/compare.py` that compares two iterations on shared tasks.
2. **Implementation:**
   - Accept iteration names or numbers: `iteration-021` or just `021` (resolve to `results/auto-improve/iteration-NNN`)
   - Load `_watch_scores.json` from both iterations
   - Find shared task IDs (intersection of both task sets)
   - For each shared task, classify as: both pass, both fail, regression (A pass → B fail), improvement (A fail → B pass)
   - Print summary table: total shared, both pass, both fail, regressions, improvements, net delta
   - Print per-task diff for regressions and improvements (task ID + status change)
   - Exit code 0 if no regressions, 1 if regressions found
3. **Wire** entry point: `python -m auto_improve.compare 021 022`

## Phase 4: Cross-Repo — Provider Routing (megaplan)

### Step 8: Provider routing consolidation (`megaplan/providers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium — requires megaplan repo access
1. **Audit** `megaplan/hermes_worker.py` and `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` for `_resolve_model()` duplicates. Verify exact function signatures and prefix handling.
2. **Extract** the shared function into `megaplan/providers.py`:
   - Handle prefix stripping: `zhipu:`, `minimax:`, `google:` → provider-specific base URL + API key
   - `~/.hermes/.env` loading
   - OpenRouter fallback
3. **Update** both `hermes_worker.py` and `parallel_critique.py` to import from `megaplan.providers`.
4. **Test** by running a single task with each provider prefix to verify routing still works.

**Note:** This step requires `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan` access which was not available during planning. The exact line numbers and function signatures need verification before implementation.

## Phase 5: Tests

### Step 9: Tests for new functionality
**Scope:** Medium

Add focused tests following existing test patterns (see `tests/test_manifest.py`, `tests/test_watch_scoring.py`, `tests/test_auto_improve_score_experiment.py`).

1. **`tests/test_manifest.py`** — add test for `requeue_dead_claimed()`:
   - Create manifest with tasks claimed by different worker PIDs
   - Write a pidfile listing one worker PID as alive (mock `_pid_alive` to control)
   - Call `requeue_dead_claimed(pidfile_path)`, verify only tasks from the dead worker are requeued
   - Test fallback: no pidfile → all claimed tasks requeued with warning
2. **`tests/test_watch_scoring.py`** — add test for modal sandbox early exhaustion:
   - Create scores_data with a task having `error_category: "modal_sandbox"` and `attempts: 2`
   - Call `find_retryable_tasks()`, verify task is in `exhausted_ids` not `retryable_ids`
   - Verify a non-modal-sandbox task at `attempts: 2` is still retryable
3. **`tests/test_auto_improve_score_experiment.py`** — add test for config-based task ordering:
   - Call `_ordered_task_ids()` with a `results_root` containing a `_run_config.json` with a subset `evals_to_run`
   - Verify it uses the config list, not the global `tasks.json`
4. **`tests/test_compare.py`** — add test for comparison tool:
   - Create two mock `_watch_scores.json` files with known overlapping tasks
   - Run comparison, verify it correctly classifies regressions/improvements
5. **Process-management tests** (kill command, scorer CLI) are smoke-tested manually — they require actual process spawning.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (manifest safety) first — it's the safety-critical fix that should land before any new iteration runs.
2. Step 2 (modal skip) — small, independent, can land immediately.
3. Steps 3-4 (PID tracking + kill command) together — kill command depends on pidfile.
4. Step 5 (scorer CLI + scoring_mode="none") — depends on Step 3 for pidfile registration.
5. Step 6 (task selection) — independent, can be done in parallel with Steps 3-5.
6. Step 7 (comparison) — independent, can land anytime.
7. Step 8 (provider routing) — independent, requires megaplan access.
8. Step 9 (tests) — write tests alongside each step, but grouped here for plan clarity.

## Validation Order
1. Step 1: Create manifest with claimed+done tasks, write a pidfile with a dead worker PID. Call `load_or_create()` + `requeue_dead_claimed()`. Confirm only dead workers' tasks requeued, done tasks untouched.
2. Step 2: Run `find_retryable_tasks` with mock `modal_sandbox` entry at 2 attempts → verify exhausted.
3. Steps 3-4: Launch a test iteration, verify `_pidfile.json` created with correct structure; run `python -m auto_improve.kill --dry-run --iteration NNN` and verify it finds the right PIDs.
4. Step 5: Verify `python -m auto_improve.score --help` works. Verify `scoring_mode="none"` in parallel.py skips all scoring. Verify `--skip-scoring` works on both `loop.py` and `run_experiment.py`. Verify scorer writes `scorer_pid` to pidfile.
5. Step 6: Run `python -m auto_improve.loop --tasks 5 --seed 42 --dry-run` and verify 5 tasks selected; verify `score_experiment.py` reads from `_run_config.json`.
6. Step 7: Compare two existing iterations (e.g., 021 vs 022).
7. Step 9: `pytest tests/test_manifest.py tests/test_watch_scoring.py tests/test_auto_improve_score_experiment.py tests/test_compare.py -v`

  "is-there-convincing-verification-for-the-change": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-062",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: the verification story is still incomplete for the parser itself. the dry run verifies field stripping, and the live run verifies that responses come back, but there is still no planned repo-local check that the keyword matcher behaves correctly on the actual saved response shape already present in the traces, including prose preambles and fenced json. that leaves the most judgment-sensitive part of the script to manual eyeballing after the fact.",

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-084",

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-105",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: checked the revised test plan against the actual validator behavior. step 7 says a placeholder-only file should fail the required-key check and fall through, but the repo\u2019s generic validation only checks key existence in `megaplan/workers.py:221-227`, and the proposed critique/finalize/review templates would already have those keys. that means the planned placeholder test is based on a false premise and would not cover the real regression where an untouched template preempts a valid text response.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-structured-output-20260401-1616"
      ]
    },
    {

  "search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-077",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked all active swe-bench scoring entry points again. `evals/watch_scoring.py:205-223` still hardcodes `--dataset_name princeton-nlp/swe-bench_verified`, while `evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py` and `evals/parallel.py:547-556` still omit dataset selection, and the revised plan still does not budget changes for those paths even though the stated goal is 'use the exact same image scoring uses'.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-078",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the revised prebuild placement against the actual worker flow. moving prebuild out of `prepare_workspace()` avoids `--prepare-eval`, but patch-only worker subprocesses still run `run_all_evals()` after `evals/parallel.py:459-463` sets `swebench_patch_only=true`, so the plan's claim that patch-only workers avoid prebuild is not true unless it also guards on patch-only mode.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-103",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: re-checked every hermes phase that uses structured output. beyond `prep`, `critique`, `finalize`, and `review`, the same tool-plus-schema pattern still exists for `plan`, `research`, `revise`, and `gate`: toolsets are assigned in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:51-62`, schemas are defined in `megaplan/workers.py:48-59`, and the handlers persist those worker payloads in `megaplan/handlers.py:594-780`. the revised plan intentionally keeps those phases out of scope, but that means the broader repository-level reliability problem is still not fully contained.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-structured-output-20260401-1616"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-121",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked megaplan/workers.py:1022-1058, megaplan/handlers.py:679-735, megaplan/_core/state.py:95-108, and megaplan/workers.py:748-780. critique execution is not hermes-only today; it goes through generic worker dispatch, agent fallback, and one persisted `*_critic` session slot, so routing `handle_critique()` straight to `run_parallel_critique()` and `run_hermes_step()` would broaden the change into agent-routing and session-state behavior that the plan does not currently budget.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "parallelize-the-critique-20260401-1840"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-125",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked `megaplan/workers.py:748-783`, `megaplan/workers.py:960-1021`, and `megaplan/_core/state.py:95-108`. the revised plan fixes the earlier agent-routing problem by gating parallelism to hermes, but it still changes critique session behavior more broadly than the current repo does: `resolve_agent_mode()` defaults critique to persistent mode, while the plan assumes `fresh=true` for all sub-agents and then records the overall parallel step as `refreshed=true`, so hermes critique would stop reusing the normal `hermes_critic` session at all unless the implementation adds an explicit replacement contract.",

        Escalated debt subsystems:
        [
  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 11,
    "plan_count": 4,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-061",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the planned response parsing against the real trace outputs. the final critique messages in the repository commonly contain prose followed by a json object, but the implementation section reduces parsing to case-insensitive substring search over the raw response text. that is weaker than the requested 'try to extract json' behavior and can miscount a keyword that appears outside the actual findings payload.",
        "occurrence_count": 2,
        "plan_ids": [
          "build-a-simple-script-scripts-20260401-0410"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-063",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised parsing approach against the real trace outputs. the repository\u2019s saved critique responses are not plain json strings; they include prose prefixes, and at least one target trace (`django__django-10973`) wraps the json in a fenced code block. a case-insensitive substring scan over the whole response can still credit a model for mentioning a keyword anywhere in wrapper text without proving the keyword appeared in the actual critique findings, so the core comparison logic remains weaker than the requested 'extract json and inspect findings' behavior.",
        "occurrence_count": 2,
        "plan_ids": [
          "build-a-simple-script-scripts-20260401-0410"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-075",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `skip_home_overlay` and `extra_run_args` changes against the actual environment creation paths. the plan only mentions `_get_env_config()` and `_create_environment()`, but both `tools/file_tools.py:109-118` and `tools/terminal_tool.py:960-967` currently build `container_config` dicts that do not carry either of those new settings, so a first `read_file` or `patch` call can still create a docker sandbox with the old `/root` overlay behavior and without the relaxed run args.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-076",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed `extra_run_args` passthrough against `tools/environments/docker.py:106-116,318`. appending extra args after `_security_args` does not explain how the existing `--tmpfs /tmp...`, `--tmpfs /var/tmp...`, and `--pids-limit 256` entries are removed or overridden, so the revised plan still does not technically solve the parity problem it claims to address.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-088",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new patch-file design against current control flow. because `evals/run_evals.py:927-944` reads the prompt before any proposed prebuild hook and `read_prompt()` still takes only `(task_name, source_root)`, the plan's idea of conditionally instructing the agent to emit `/hermes_output/model_patch.diff` is not wired to the actual runtime order and remains technically under-specified.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-101",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised required-key validation idea against the real validators. in this repo `validate_payload()` only checks top-level key presence in `megaplan/workers.py:221-227`, while the proposed critique template would already contain `checks`, `flags`, `verified_flag_ids`, and `disputed_flag_ids`, and `_schema_template()` already emits all required top-level keys for finalize and review in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:510-538`. an untouched or half-filled template file would therefore still be accepted as the primary payload, then fail later in `validate_critique_checks()` (`megaplan/checks.py:179-220`), `_validate_finalize_payload()` (`megaplan/handlers.py:277-303`), or review merging (`megaplan/handlers.py:989-1025`) without any planned fallthrough back to valid text json. the earlier incomplete-file concern remains open.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-structured-output-20260401-1616"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-102",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked template fidelity after the revision. the plan now fixes critique by switching to `build_empty_template(checks)`, but it still proposes `_schema_template()` for finalize and review even though that helper collapses nested objects to `{}` and nested arrays to `[]` in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:510-538`. for the two most complex remaining phases, that still gives the model a placeholder file with no task/verdict structure to edit, so the prior shallow-template concern is only partially resolved.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-structured-output-20260401-1616"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-124",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `megaplan/flags.py:57-65` against the new merge rule that unions `verified_flag_ids` and `disputed_flag_ids` across sub-agents. the plan still does not define a conflict policy when different check agents return the same flag id in both lists; today `update_flags_after_critique()` marks verified first and disputed second without clearing the `verified` fields, so one merged contradiction can leave a flag in inconsistent state (`verified: true` but `status: \"disputed\"`).",
        "occurrence_count": 2,
        "plan_ids": [
          "parallelize-the-critique-20260401-1840"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.

Read this file first — it contains 4 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if it's a concern, false if not

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

This is critique iteration 3. The template file includes prior findings with their status. Verify addressed flags were actually fixed, re-flag if inadequate, and check for new issues introduced by the revision.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.
